Prospect Research · Florida
Draft · 2026-09-18 · rev 5
One tab per artist · 145 artists
FL

Prepared by Keyman · internal prospect research

Florida seed prospects145 Florida-based independents from a Tallahassee client’s related-artist graph, each with an MLC check on the hit

Spotify’s related-artist graph was walked out from the client’s profile in two passes (3,906 profiles measured). A profile is kept when the artist’s own bio names a Florida place, or when all three of its top listener cities are in Florida. The pool is the 145 that lead their own hit, with a hit of at least 300,000 Spotify plays and at most 400,000 monthly listeners. 27 feature-only profiles and 22 major-tier Florida names were set aside. One tab per artist; registry data as at 2026-09-16.

98 of 145 show a mechanical gap at The MLC — 57 no work · 40 partly claimed · 1 ISRC on another song · 19 duplicate/review · 28 fully claimed. 43 of the 98 gap leads state Florida in their own bio.

321,763,396 of the 742,336,265 measured plays sit on hits with an open item. Plays are usage counts on the recordings, never an entitlement figure.

Basis and limits. Internal prospect research from public data: Spotify artist pages, the Deezer catalog and The MLC public work search (exact ISRC first, artist + recording title for the misses, then each work’s record). A positive control was run before the pass and every call’s error state recorded; no row errored. “No MLC work found” is absence in the public search, not proof of absence. Florida placement is confirmed only from the artist’s own Spotify bio; “listener base” rows use Spotify’s top-listener cities, which are audience geography, not origin. A registration is a set of payment instructions, never payment itself. BMI, ASCAP, GMR and SoundExchange were not searched. No filing, amendment or claim has been made at any registry. Since “timeless music should always be the main objective.” Inspired by his past, present and future, Dayou aims to produce crystal clear stories that assume the role of a scrapbook. From millions of streams on SoundCloud, a surplus of 100K monthly listeners on Spotify, as well as working with producers like the Pyrvmids (cred: Willow Smith, Denzel Curry), the creative aims to inspire others through his artistry. Follow Dayou on Instagram and Twitter - @dayouchsn [email removed]

About (Spotify)

NHL Reece built a significant fan base in the late 2010s and early 2020s with his various blends of hip-hop. After working his way into the spotlight with a series of songs and videos, the rapper found his biggest hit to date with 2019's "My Own" which has gained over 7.8M streams via Soundcloud, which has led him into rejecting three major label deals. Born and raised in Central Beachside Florida, NHL Reece made his debut into the rap game as OG Reece with 2018's "Emoji's" accompanied by frequent collaborator NHL Tahz. It was in late 2018 when he adopted the stage name NHL Reece under his own brand "NeighborHood Legends". Kicking off the new decade NHL Reece dropped the highly anticipated visual to "My Own" and has been consistent since. He recently signed a distribution deal with "Hitmaker Services" and dropped "Brighter Day" through them.

About (Spotify)

The very first song rapper Flippa ever made resulted in a video with more than 7 million streams. In 2014, the longtime Migos affiliate took his close friends’ advice and put his experiences, charisma, and style to music. It has led to a series of celebrated videos, albums, and smash singles with Rich The Kid, Lil Yachty, and Lil Durk. Originally from Palm Beach, Florida, the rapper known as Skippa Da Flippa moved to Gwinnett County, Georgia in his early teens. “Quavo and Takeoff were the first people that I talked to when I came out here,” he says of the area northeast of the ATL. Quavo brought his right-hand man Flippa to Gucci Mane as well as the would-be team at Quality Control. “The first thing Gucci said to me is ‘Stay out of jail. It’s no place for a Black man.’” Instead of returning to the trap, Flippa became a fixture at Migos’ studio and shows, part of the Y.R.N. movement. Rath…

About (Spotify)

Broward, Florida producer Nuez started producing and DJ’ing at the age of 12 after discovering his fathers vinyl collection and saving money to buy himself an MPC. Eventually he grew his name as a producer in South Florida and started engineering recording sessions in 2012. After interning at a Studio Center in Miami, Nuez partnered up with a recording studio where he would call home for the next 3 years. While working at this studio he built relationships with many of the artists that would record with him. Since then he has come to work with many of Florida’s great underground artists completing multiple studio projects and albums. The first studio album that he fully produced and engineered ended up being picked up and distributed by 300 Ent. (Year of the Savage - Robb Banks) in 2015. More recently, Nuez has branched out to building his own artist career as producer. He began working …

About (Spotify)

Although they calling me rookie I sounds like I’ve been in the game for a long time; it’s just the quality of the music I’m making,” declares 17-year-old rapper Chibi LOL. The Dallas, Texas native of Nigerian heritage used his 2020 to make a powerful introduction. His Fresh Ayr-produced Pain-Tence mixtape yielded the “Life Is Short” music video as well as melodic-yet-personal tracks like “Two Face” and “Easy To Die”—all achieving strong six figures at streaming. Currently wrapping up his debut album, Chibi is all-in on music. Chibi translates to “heaven-sent,” adding the initial for young. The idea of divinity is vital to the streetwise rapper. Chibi Okapara and his older brother grew up in North Dallas’ Josey Lane Apartments. When Chibi was 12, his father left the family and returned to Africa. Two years later, he died, right as Chibi was becoming a man. Music became a coping mechanism.…

North Carolina rapper J.K. The Reaper aligns provoking lyricism with 808 engulfed hi-hats and synth melodies that typically induce simplicity, much like contemporaries Joey BadA$$, Denzel Curry, and Earl Sweatshirt. Born Jabril Kenan, he first found a passion for hip-hop through his father's all genre-encompassing, record collection. A home life where music discovery was the ultimate currency undoubtedly influenced the development of J.K. The Reaper’s knack for putting rhymes and beats together, with unshakable inspiration drawn from the likes of Nas and Outkast. Before acknowledging ‘The Reaper’ within, illustrious dark and intellectual lyrics drew internet fans in through a stream of Soundcloud releases he recorded in his childhood bedroom. About (Spotify)

Roc Nation’s Bobby Fishscale is a hip-hop artist who has established himself as one of the most promising rappers to come out of Florida with his street anthems. In 2019, he made waves with his track “Hov Flow,” that sampled JAY-Z and Eminem’s “Renegade” beat, which grabbed attention from popular hip-hop platforms and blogs. His debut mixtape - Big Fish – released that year – ended up stirring a bidding war amongst record labels that culminated in his eventual signing to Roc Nation. Starting a new chapter in 2020, Fishscale burst into the hip-hop scene with his standout single “Blue Cheese.” Shortly after, he released his project The Last Re-Up, which featured appearances from the likes of 1017 veteran Peeway Longway, Dreamchaser’s Tafia, veteran Bigga Rankin and many more. The project also includes fan-favorite single “No 9 to 5” which has garnered over 1M+ views on YouTube to date. The…

Skeet McFlurry is an American rapper, singer, and songwriter known for his captivating lyricism and storytelling ability. Growing up in Jacksonville, Florida, he experienced a troublesome adolescence but found solace in writing music. Skeet honed his craft in the bunk room of his grandmother's home, where he developed a unique sound that would later become his trademark. Between 2014 and 2018, Skeet released five independent mixtapes locally and online, which helped him build a loyal following. His debut album, Mr. McFlurry, released in July 2019, was a critical and commercial success, paving the way for future projects. About (Spotify)

Tokyo Jetz is a rapper from Jacksonville, Florida who initially received attention over a series of freestyle videos recorded in her car. Her style is brash, aggressive, and dramatic, with lyrics focusing on the harsh aspects of street life, but she can also be playful as well as introspective. Since debuting in 2016, she has collaborated with rappers like T.I., Trina, and Kash Doll. Her third album, Cancel Culture, appeared in 2021. Originally a poet, Tokyo Jetz (born Shauntrell Pender) began rapping while in high school. Gradually, her freestyles and mixtape appearances attracted attention from several big-name rappers, including T.I., who signed her to Grand Hustle in 2016. Jetz moved to Atlanta in early 2017 and toured the United States with her labelmates. Following the upbeat, summery single "Sunshine Baby" and the reflective "My Story," her label debut, Viral, appeared in August 2…

About (Spotify)

Richy Samo, also known as Richysamo, was born and raised in Miami, Florida. Starting as an early DJ/producer on the underground scene, Samo quickly gained notoriety. It wasn't until the murder of Samo's brother that he began making rap music a career. In fact, his brother's last words to him were, "Stay out the streets and take this music shit serious". Shortly thereafter, he linked up with Lil Pump, Smokepurpp , and YOPPA BAM to create the rap group Gucci Gang. He even went on to collaborate with XXXTENTACION. Richy Samo was quickly scooped up by the label Alamo Record and began hitting the studio hard. After critically acclaimed songs like The Hated, Bang, and Trap Baby, Samo is scheduled to release the full length project "SkrewHead" In December 2018 and shows no signs of slowing down. About (Spotify)

Miami rapper Mike Smiff steadily built a reputation on the Dade County scene with a series of mixtapes in the 2010s. The rapper flipped between solo releases, like 2013's 10 Toes Down, and collaborative tapes such as 2012's Table Manners: We Gone Eat by Any Means Necessary, Vol. 2, which he made with Big Mann. In 2017, and with a substantial number of releases under his belt, Smiff signed to the Miami hip-hop label Slip-n-Slide. Later that year, he released the 17-track Chase Dis Money, followed by a guest-spot on labelmate Trick Daddy's single "Paradise." ~ Bekki Bemrose

Hailing from North Miami, Florida, Eighty8 began his musical journey at a young age, discovering an early passion for singing before evolving into a versatile and compelling force in the music scene. Born in Virginia and raised in the trenches of Saint Petersburg, Florida, independent rap artist, James Jones aka Jimbo World, is rapidly making his mark in the rap game. He credits close friends for his moniker—“Jimbo”, a play on the childhood nickname, Jimmy, and “World” was added because he presents views according to his own life, way, and world. Jimbo World’s music, often described as “blood raw”, tells stories of his struggle, his family and his rise from the street life. Raised by his mother and beloved grandmother, Jimbo World experienced life without his father, who was incarcerated in 1993. About (Spotify)

Raised by a single mother, music surrounded Rellyski from a young age. His Grandma regularly brought Rellyski to church where he honed his voice in choir rehearsals. Meanwhile, mom sang all the time, especially on Sunday “cleanup day blasting Mary J. Blige.” Even though Rellyski listened to everything from R&B to rap, he initially gravitated towards football. Shining on the field as a receiver and free safety, he wound up at Heidelberg University in Ohio. Beyond D3 football, he recorded demos at the school radio station. Unfortunately, financial stress forced him to return to Florida. At 19-years-old, he held down jobs at a call center and Target. Rellyski's skill was unstoppable. He uploaded “FOREAL” to Soundcloud, where Say Cheese championed it catching the attention of Bad Habit. “They flew me out to L.A. to talk, and everything changed,” he recalls. About (Spotify)

Breadwinna GDawg just wants to have fun. Since the age of 12, the Florida native, born Travis Williams, began recording infectious songs mainly made strictly for his inner circle. But in 2018, his thumping breakout song, “Big B’s,” attracted listeners with its thumping, Dirty South-influenced sound. After facing legal troubles, he followed his growing success with “First Day Out.” His signature style is reminiscent of fellow Florida MC, Kodak Black, Boosie and DaBaby as it blends the high energy and charisma of southern showmen. Today, Breadwinna pumps a slate of new material, including “Say Dat,” “Jumped Off The Porch,” and “My Whoodie” with lyrics about gunplay, girls and his ever-present stories from the streets. His latest single, “Offset and Cardi” off his forthcoming project, 4 Headed Goat, will usher in new music for 2020.

Considered by some to be "superhuman," Chad Thomas, aka Major Nine, is a rapper-producer who also played defensive end for the Cleveland Browns. • Thomas grew up in the Liberty City neighborhood of Miami. His family emphasized the importance of music and sports, and as a result, he excelled at football and learned to play nine instruments, including the piano, trombone, trumpet, and tuba. • Major Nine cowrote and produced Rick Ross' 2017 track “Apple of My Eye,” featuring Raphael Saadiq. • In 2018, he was selected in the third round of the NFL Draft by the Cleveland Browns. • He’s released a string of full-length projects, including 2018’s Big Tape, 2019’s Lil Tape II, and 2020’s Unconditional, Chizzim Tape, and No Pain, No Glory. • In 2020, Major Nine featured on “Trap Back,” off Chris Brown and Young Thug’s joint mixtape Slime & B..

About (Spotify)

When he was just 17, Bronx rapper Justin Rarri had a viral hit with his effortlessly catchy single "W2LEEZY." He brought similarly narcotic flows and bass-forward beats to his 2019 mixtape 4EVARARRi. Justin Rarri was born in Miami, Florida in 2002 but spent his formative years in the Bronx. He was rapping in his teens and in 2018 released his "Opps" single as well as appearing in features on tracks by other artists. His 2019 single "W2LEEZY" was a breakthrough for him, with its video viewed millions of times in just a few months and the song hitting millions on streaming services as well. This overnight buzz caught the attention of major labels, and Rarri soon signed with Interscope. Before 2019 was up, the rapper released several other successful singles, a remix of "W2LEEZY" featuring an appearance from YK Osiris, and a nine-track mixtape titled 4EVARARRi. ~ Fred Thomas, Rovi

About (Spotify)

A member of Atlanta rapper Young Jeezy's coke-rap trio U.S.D.A., Panama City, FL, native Blood Raw (b. Bruce Falson) began his rap career while serving a six-year bid in Florida prison during the mid-‘90s. There he first developed his rhyme skills and earned the "Blood Raw" moniker (which is local slang for "the truth"). Upon his release in 1998, he relocated to Atlanta to work for his uncle at a local rap hotspot called the Hip-Hop Café. Inspired by the many up-and-coming acts that graced the venue, Blood Raw moved back to Panama City in 1999 to start a rap crew of his own. He eventually crossed paths with Young Jeezy performing at the same show as he in small town Dothan, AL. Deflecting interest from T.I.'s Grand Hustle imprint, Asylum, and other labels, Blood Raw joined Jeezy's U.S.D.A. outfit and signed with the Atlanta rapper's Corporate Thugz Entertainment (CTE) in 2004. About (Spotify)

On melodic rap phenomenon Cico P’s breakout single “Tampa,” he smoothly glides from verses to hook with a confidence and swagger that few possess in the current hip-hop landscape. The Jacksonville, Texas native is rising up the ranks as a promising new upstart with a mission to add his own charismatic flavor to hip-hop. It was through his father that he learned about Texas artists like UGK and the Swisha House roster, mixed with 50 Cent, Snoop Dogg, DMX, and Bone Thugs-n-Harmony, while his mom introduced him to R&B acts like Omarion, Tyrese, and 112. That combination of hard raps and melodies inspired him to make his own music. He dropped his first project Sweet Union in 2019, fueled by the song “Tampa,” which has nothing to do with Florida, despite his hometown of Jacksonville, TX sharing the same name as the Floridian geotag. About (Spotify)

Deriving from the Greek god of beauty and desire, Sinoda is the stage name of Houston, Texas based artist Tryston Obevoen, choosing "Sinoda" as a way of representing today's society idolizing backward desires. Growing up in El Paso, Texas, Sinoda was fascinated by the R&B/Soul genre while also experimenting with minimal hip-hop. He learned to play piano and guitar from an early age, becoming self-taught in singing as well along with learning drums, bass, and ukulele. Taking his talents to the recording studio in 2019, Sinoda is an up and coming artist bringing to the table a multi-genre sound, with songs ranging from Rock and Roll, to Country, to RNB. About (Spotify)

LaTheGoat is a songwriter and lyricist from Tampa, Florida, who was discovered by Jermaine Dupri in 2020. LA's first single "8 Bands" hit with fans, so JD brought him to Def Jam, added Rick Ross to the remix, and sparked the start of a something big for the young talent. LaTheGoat followed up with "You Wouldn't Believe Me" and "Bounce It" featuring Big Freedia, both produced by Jermaine Dupri.
